What if I speak multiple languages?

For meeting reports, Read will automatically detect which language is being spoken primarily during the meeting and then use that language when generating the transcript and notes. Meetings where multiple different languages are spoken are not fully supported by Read and may produce inconsistent results. If you have a preferred language, you can set that language as the Output Language in your settings here: https://app.read.ai/analytics/settings/user/report-content For Readouts, Read will automatically detect the language used across all of your connected sources, and if the predominant language is not a supported language, no Readouts will be generated. #### What if my language isn’t supported? We intend to continue expanding support of all features to more languages. If you have a specific request for language support, please contact Read Support, as our team takes user demand into account when deciding which languages to prioritize.

What should I say when an attendee asks me what is Read Assistant?

Everyone will use Read in a way that is unique to them so we are providing 10 examples of what you could say when an attendee asks you what is Read Assistant. Feel free to choose one or several from the below list, or if this inspires you to come up with your own, go for it! 1. “I added Read to keep an eye on how much I speak in the meeting to ensure I allow room for others to participate as well.” 2. “I know that meetings often go over so I added Read to help keep track of how much time is left in the meeting.” 3. “I added Read to help me improve the way I collaborate with others.” 4. “I added Read to the call to take notes so that I can focus on the conversation.” 5. “I added Read to the call to get a better understanding of how participants react to what I’m saying in the meeting.” 6. “I added Read so I can receive post meeting analytics on engagement and sentiment for the topics we discuss.” 7. “I added Read to help me keep track of how much time I’m spending in meetings with individuals on my team.” 8. “I want to make sure I’m not talking too much in my meetings so I add Read to all my meetings to measure my total talk time.” 9. “I want to understand how to make my meetings better so I add Read to measure how my meetings are going overall.” 10. “I add Read to all my calls to see how often I’m late for anyone I meet with so I can make changes to be more timely.”

Why is my report showing as “out of quota”?

Users on a Free Read plan may notice that some meeting reports are unavailable, and are instead marked with an “Out of quota” message at the top: On the Reports listing page it will say “Free plan quota exceeded”: If this happens to you, it means that you have reached your monthly limit of free reports (which is 5 for Free users and 10 for Zoom Apps users) and will need to upgrade your plan if you want to use Read for upcoming meetings later in the month. Reports you own, as well as reports that have been shared with you, count against your monthly report limit. Once you’ve reached your limit, you will need to upgrade to a paid plan in order to unlock reports that are out of quota. When you upgrade your plan, Read will go back and automatically reprocess all of your reports from the past 30 days to apply the features of your new plan (which includes unlocking reports that were out of quota when upgrading from free, and enabling video playback when upgrading to Enterprise). #### When does my quota reset? You can view your quota reset date on your Plan & Billing page. #### Can I view an out of quota report? Yes – if you upgrade your plan to one of Read’s paid plans, and the meeting occurred within the past 30 days. In addition to getting access to the plan going forward, as a courtesy Read will also unlock the features of that plan for all reports over the past 30 days. So if you want access to the video, for example, you should upgrade to the Enterprise plan instead of the Pro plan. Just be aware that this will only work for meetings that the Read Assistant joined and successfully recorded (so for example, it won’t work for any reports on your Incomplete page). #### Why am I seeing this if I paid for Read? All of Read’s paid plans have unlimited monthly meeting reports. Please double check that it does not say “Free” or “Zoom Apps” for your current plan, either in the top left of your menu or on the Plan & Billing page: If you’re not seeing your expected plan here: - Make sure that you’re logged in to the correct account. Sometimes users accidentally create multiple Read accounts and are logged in to the wrong one. - Check the invoices listed in your Billing History on the Plan & Billing page to make sure there are no failed invoices. If you have a failed payment and you’re not sure why, double check that your payment method is correct and valid, and if you need further assistance, you can contact Read Support.

Getting Advanced with Read

About Sentiment, Engagement, and the Read Score

  • What is the Read Score?
  • What is Sentiment and how is it measured?
  • What is Engagement and how is it measured?
  • Scoring benchmarks
  • Overall vs Participant scores

What is the Read Score?

The Read Score (previously called the Meeting Score) provides an overall metric on how well your meeting is going by combining Read’s analysis of Sentiment and Engagement. By analyzing facial and verbal elements of all meeting attendees, Read assesses how people are reacting (positively, neutrally or negatively) as well as their level of involvement and interest in real time. This provides all meeting attendees with a scientific measure of the meeting’s effectiveness so you can drive to a better meeting experience together. (image: Screen_Shot_2022-04-19_at_1.44.39_PM.png) Please Note: For users in the EU and UK, the Read Score does not include any use of meeting video or facial elements to calculate a score. All users can disable affective metrics (Sentiment, Engagement, and the Read Score) at any time in Account Settings. Once disabled, these scores will no longer be calculated or shown in reports. #### What is Sentiment and how is it measured? Sentiment measures how positively or negatively attendees are feeling throughout a meeting. Read analyzes both facial and verbal cues from attendees to determine whether they are expressing positive, neutral or negative reactions to the meeting in real time. For example, if you are presenting a slide to a large group and can’t see the facial expressions of all meeting attendees, Read can detect that people are smiling and nodding their heads. Sentiment levels will begin to increase, indicating to you that your audience is reacting positively to your content. (image: Screen_Shot_2022-04-19_at_1.45.48_PM.png) Read uses well-established methodologies and proprietary models to analyze facial and verbal elements, such as facial expressions, head movements and body language. Read also analyzes the overall content of the speech (for example, if people are using positive or negative words and phrases) with natural language processing (NLP), a type of machine learning that allows computers to understand the meaning behind human language. #### What is Engagement and how is it measured? Engagement is a measure of attendees’ level of involvement and interest in a meeting. We measure engagement through a combination of facial and verbal cues, as well as the talk time of attendees throughout the meeting, to assess whether engagement levels are high, medium or low in real time. For example, if you are in a team meeting and people start to get distracted, Read will notice that they are looking away and leaning back from the screen. Engagement levels will begin to decrease, indicating to all meeting participants to refocus on the meeting. (image: Screen_Shot_2022-04-19_at_1.47.25_PM.png) Read uses well-established methodologies and proprietary models to analyze facial and verbal elements, such as facial expressions, head movements and body language, as well as the pitch, volume and intonation of participants’ speech. The proportional talk time is also considered. #### Scoring benchmarks (image: Screenshot 2024-07-18 at 11.43.30 PM.png) The various scores are graded on a scale of Good, Okay, and Poor: - Good: greater than 80 - Okay: 70-80 - Poor: less than 70

You can also see the average score for all Read users as another point of comparison. #### Overall vs Participant scores So far, this article has focused on the overall Sentiment, Engagement, and Read Scores, which are based on an average of measurements from all meeting participants. You can also view your own personal scores for various metrics on the Deep Dive tab of a report, under Standings: The Participant Score is just the individual version of the Read score. Below it you can see your own personal Engagement and Sentiment score. All scores also show the overall meeting scores (“All Participants”) and the Read user average across all meetings on the same chart for comparison. In most cases, the Participant Score will be within the range of either the Sentiment or Engagement number.  However, there are exceptions when Engagement is high (e.g. 80+), where it will amplify the Sentiment score, resulting in a higher Participant Score. Two examples: 1. Within Range Example: If you heard another team at your company was going to get a bonus, you might have high sentiment (82) as its great news for the company, but since you aren’t directly on that team, you aren’t as focused and have engagement of (75).  Your Participant Score would be in the range of 75 to 82. 2. Outside of Range Example: If your team was getting the bonus, you might have a higher sentiment (88) and higher engagement (89), the higher engagement amplifies the Participant Score and you end up at 92.

Ada - Email Authentication Required (SPF, DKIM, DMARC)

When you email Ada, we verify that your message is actually coming from your email domain — not from someone pretending to be you. If your domain’s email authentication isn’t properly configured, your message won’t be delivered and you’ll receive an auto-reply directing you to this page. #### Why does Read require email authentication? Read AI’s email-based assistant processes requests on your behalf — things like scheduling, retrieving information, and coordinating with others. Because the assistant acts on instructions received via email, it’s critical that we can verify the sender’s identity. Email authentication protocols (SPF, DKIM, and DMARC) are the industry standard for proving that an email genuinely came from the domain it claims to come from. Without these protections, a bad actor could send emails that appear to come from your address and instruct the assistant to take actions you never authorized. To prevent this, Read drops any inbound message from a sending domain that fails SPF, DKIM, or DMARC validation. This protects you and your organization from spoofing and impersonation. #### What are SPF, DKIM, and DMARC? These three protocols work together to authenticate email: SPF (Sender Policy Framework) defines which mail servers are authorized to send email on behalf of your domain. It’s configured as a DNS TXT record. DKIM (DomainKeys Identified Mail) adds a cryptographic signature to outgoing emails, allowing the recipient to verify the message wasn’t altered in transit. It also requires a DNS TXT record. DMARC (Domain-based Message Authentication, Reporting & Conformance) ties SPF and DKIM together with a policy that tells receiving servers what to do when authentication fails. It’s published as a DNS TXT record and requires at least one of SPF or DKIM to be properly configured first. #### How to configure email authentication for your domain Setting up these records requires access to your domain’s DNS settings. If you’re not sure who manages your domain’s DNS, check with your IT team or domain registrar. Here are trusted guides for getting each protocol configured: - SPF: Google Workspace — Set up SPF · Microsoft 365 — Set up SPF - DKIM: Google Workspace — Turn on DKIM · Microsoft 365 — Set up DKIM - DMARC: Google Workspace — Set up DMARC · Microsoft 365 — Set up DMARC

If you use a different email provider, check their documentation for instructions on setting up SPF, DKIM, and DMARC. You can also use free tools like MXToolbox or Google Admin Toolbox to verify your current configuration. #### What to do after making changes Once your domain’s SPF, DKIM, and DMARC records are properly configured: 1. Allow time for DNS propagation. DNS changes can take anywhere from a few minutes to 48 hours to fully propagate, though most updates take effect within an hour. 2. Verify your records. Use MXToolbox to confirm your SPF, DKIM, and DMARC records are resolving correctly. 3. Re-send your original email. Read does not automatically retry messages that were previously dropped. You’ll need to send a new email to the assistant to start your conversation.

If you’ve confirmed your email authentication is properly configured and are still receiving this error, please contact us at support@read.ai.

Ada - Meeting Intelligence & Preparation

What meeting-related questions can Ada answer?

Ada has access to all meetings that Read AI has captured. This means Ada can help with questions like: - “What did we decide in last week’s project sync?” - “What action items came out of my meeting with Sarah on Monday?” - “What was the timeline we agreed on for the Q3 launch?” - “Summarize what I missed in the team standup yesterday” - “What topics have I discussed with [client name] in our last few meetings?”

Ada doesn’t just search for keywords, it reasons about the question, finds the relevant discussion across transcripts, and synthesizes an answer. #### Do I need Ask Read enabled in order for Ada to answer meeting questions? No. Meeting intelligence is available to any Read AI user whose meetings are captured by Read AI. Ada accesses meeting transcripts, summaries, action items, and key decisions directly from the Read AI platform. Ask Read is a separate, additional capability that extends Ada’s knowledge to non-meeting data sources (email, chat, documents, CRM, etc.). #### Can Ada help me prepare for upcoming meetings? Yes. Ada can surface relevant context to help you prepare for upcoming meetings, such as: - What was discussed in previous meetings with the same participants - Outstanding action items related to those participants or topics - Key decisions from related past conversations - Relevant context from the your connected knowledge sources (if Ask Read is set up)

Ada may proactively suggest things you can do with recent meeting content, such as reviewing action items, drafting follow-up emails, or scheduling follow-up meetings. #### Does Ada know which meetings I was in vs. other people’s meetings? Ada only has access to meetings associated with your Read AI account. It respects access permissions — Ada can only look at content from people who are already on the email thread and have opted in to Read AI. #### What if I ask Ada about a meeting that Read AI didn’t capture? Ada can only answer questions about meetings that Read AI has processed. This includes meetings where the Read AI bot joined the call as well as meetings recorded via the Read AI desktop or mobile app (e.g., in-person meetings or calls where the bot wasn’t used). If a meeting wasn’t captured through any of these methods, Ada won’t have that transcript or summary and will acknowledge that it doesn’t have information about that particular meeting. #### Can Ada extract and track action items from meetings? Yes. Ada can surface action items that were identified in meeting transcripts. You can ask Ada about your action items, and Ada can help with follow-ups — for example, offering to schedule time to complete a task or send a follow-up email.

Ada - Privacy, security & Data Access

Privacy, Security & Data Access

What data does Ada have access to?

Ada has access to: - The user’s meeting transcripts, summaries, action items, and key decisions from Read AI — this is Ada’s core knowledge base and requires no additional setup beyond having Read AI capture meetings - The user’s calendar (for scheduling) - Email history, chat messages, documents, and CRM data via Ask Read integrations (20+ native integrations) — only if the user has set up Ask Read - The user’s preferences/memory (stored from natural language instructions)

Ada can only access content from people who are already on the email thread and have opted in to Read AI. ##### How does Ada protect against spoofing or unauthorized access? Ada verifies email identity using SPF, DKIM, and DMARC. A person cannot send an email pretending to be another person and get Ada to respond. If email authentication fails (SPF/DKIM/DMARC failure), messages may be dropped or the user may be notified via a sidebar. ##### Is Ada SOC 2 and HIPAA compliant? Read AI is SOC 2 Type 2 compliant and Ada operates within Read AI’s security framework. Data protection includes TLS for all external communication, KMS encryption at rest, and encrypted databases. Ada is not available for users in a HIPAA workspace. While they can be on an email thread with Ada, it will never access their content, so Ada would effectively treat them as non-Read users. ##### Does Read AI train on customer data? No. Read AI is opt-out by default and does not train models against customer data unless customers explicitly opt in to the Read Customer Experience Improvement Program. ##### Can someone on an email thread who is NOT a Read user see my private data through Ada? No. Ada will never share your private information with others without your explicit confirmation via the sidebar approval flow. Ada can only access content from authenticated participants on the thread who have opted in. #### The Sidebar You might wonder if Ada will ever communicate on your behalf without checking with you first. To answer that, we will talk about the Sidebar, Ada’s approval mechanism. Before sending any email that shares potentially sensitive information or content generated from Ask Read, Ada moves the conversation out of the public thread into a private, direct email with the user (the “Original” or “OG”). In this sidebar, Ada shows a draft and asks for approval before sending. ##### When does Ada use the sidebar? Ada uses the sidebar for: - Any interaction outside of scheduling that involves sharing content from the user’s knowledge base - Ask Read-generated responses — Ada never responds with content from your transcripts, documents, or connected data without showing you the draft first - Situations where Ada is uncertain about the appropriate response

NOTE: For straightforward scheduling interactions (proposing times, confirming meetings), Ada typically acts directly without sidebaring. ##### Can Ada ever respond without user approval? Ada has a hard constraint: it cannot respond with content without the user’s explicit approval. For scheduling-only interactions (proposing times from the user’s calendar), Ada can respond more autonomously. But the core rule is: Ada can never share private content (transcripts, emails, chats, etc.) without showing you a draft and getting approval first.

Ada - Scheduling

Ada can handle your calendar for you, saving you back and forth, even across different time zones. Give Ada your scheduling preferences and it will handle the rest. #### How does scheduling with Ada work? The typical flow: 1. After a user has connected a calendar for Ada, they (or someone emailing the user) can CC’s Ada on a scheduling request, or ask Ada to schedule a meeting. 2. Ada checks the user’s calendar for availability. 3. Ada proposes available times to the other participants on the thread. 4. Once a time is confirmed, Ada creates the calendar event and sends the invite.

What scheduling defaults does Ada use?

Ada defaults to business hours, weekdays, and reasonable notice. Specifically: - Ada prefers 24-48 hour lead time - No weekend meetings unless the user explicitly says so - In a majority of cases Ada will default to scheduling 30 minute meetings unless specificed - Ada selects a default meeting app based on the user’s calendar integration and sends a conferencing link

These defaults protect users from overly aggressive scheduling. Users can override any of these by emailing Ada with their preferences in natural language. #### FAQs ##### Can Ada handle multi-person scheduling across different organizations? Yes. Ada supports multi-domain scheduling with three distinct participant groups: internal domain users, known external contacts, and unknown external contacts. When checking availability, Ada looks at every calendar it has access to. When no mutual availability exists across all participants, Ada looks at partial subsets, preferring times that work for the primary calendar owner. ##### Can Ada handle recurring meetings? Basic recurring meeting functionality exists but may have edge cases. ##### Can Ada reschedule or cancel meetings? Yes - Ada can reschedule and cancel meetings. ##### What happens if there’s a scheduling conflict? Ada proactively detects calendar conflicts and alerts the user, asking what they’d like to do. When a specific proposed time is not available, Ada examines who has the conflict and may offer to double-book over certain meetings (with intelligence about the type of meeting being considered for double-booking, e.g., internal recurring meetings may be more flexible).

Ada - Troubleshooting & Common Issues

Ada isn’t responding to emails. What should I check?

Ada replies anytime between 1 to 5 minutes. If you have not received a reply from Ada after 5 minutes, it could be due to the any of the following reasons: 1. Does the user have a Read AI account? Ada requires an active account. 2. Is ada@read.ai** on the email thread? Ada must be CC’d or directly emailed. 3. SPF/DKIM/DMARC issues**

If the sender’s email domain fails authentication, messages may be dropped. 4. More information about it here:  Email Authentication Required (SPF, DKIM, DMARC) 5. Ada was removed from the thread Ada cannot continue a task when it has been removed from the thread.

Ada scheduled a meeting at the wrong time. What should I do?

  1. Users can delete the incorrect calendar event
  2. Users can reply to Ada on the thread to correct the error and request rescheduling.
  3. Users can use the sidebar to provide guidance and redirect Ada.

Ada incorporates feedback immediately and applies it to future interactions. If this is a systemic issue (e.g., time zone consistently wrong), please reach out to support@read.ai #### Ada shared information it shouldn’t have. What happened? It’s incredibly unlikely for that to occur, there is likely a logical reason for that to occur. For example, not all users realize that they had access to certain data. #### Ada gave an incorrect or hallucinated answer. What should I do? Ada is AI-powered and will sometimes get things wrong. Our philosophy is to make mistakes visible, correctable, and instructive: 1. You can correct Ada directly in the email thread or via the sidebar. Ada will incorporate corrections immediately. 2. If the issue seems systematic (repeated hallucinations, wrong sources), please reach out to support@read.ai with screenshots and thread details (optional).

My email to Ada is being dropped / not received. What could be wrong?

This is most commonly caused by SPF/DKIM/DMARC authentication failures on the your domain. When your email fails these checks, Ada may drop the message to prevent spoofing. You should check with your IT team to ensure your domain’s email authentication records are properly configured. We have an article for your IT team: - Email Authentication Required (SPF, DKIM, DMARC)

How can I opt out of Ada’s proactive emails?

You can tell Ada your preferences directly via email , for example: - Reply with “Stop sending me proactive emails about my calendar”.

Ada would remember this from now on. #### I am on a thread and I don’t want Ada involved. What can I do? The user who “owns” Ada controls whether Ada is on a thread. Other participants cannot independently remove Ada. The owner can: 1. Remove Ada from the CC line on subsequent emails. 2. Tell Ada to stop participating in the thread. 3. Adjust their preferences to limit Ada’s proactive behavior.

Add or remove reports from folders

You can add or remove meeting reports from folders in several places throughout Read AI. This flexibility allows you to organize your reports efficiently, whether you’re working from a list view, a specific report, or managing a folder directly. #### Where to Add or Remove Reports 1. From the Reports page: In the “Folders” column, select the “Folder” dropdown and choose the desired folder(s). You can also choose to create a new folder. Select “Apply to all meetings in series” to add all past and future meetings automatically to the folder. To remove this report from the folder, unselect the checkbox next to the folder name.

  1. From the individual Report page: Select the “folder” icon to open the list and select/deselect folders to add or remove the current report. You can also apply this change to all meetings in the series, or create a new folder from this view.

  2. Individual Folder page: You can add report to a particular folder from its dedicated page, by selecting “Add Reports”. Users can add reports to it by clicking “Add Reports”. A pop-up will show a list with lite filtering options to search and choose reports to add.

To remove a report, use the overflow menu (three dots) next to the report and select “Remove From Folder.” This action removes the report from the folder but does not delete the report itself. #### Automating Folder Updates You can use Ask Read to automate folder updates for you. You can ask it to do things like “move all of my meetings with someone at acme.com to the Acme folder” or “move all meetings with ‘Kick-off call’ in the title to a Customer Kick Off Calls folder”. 

You can also ask it to do this on a schedule, for example - “every day at 9am, check for new meetings with someone from acme.com. Move those to the Acme folder”. Once you’ve set up a schedule, you can view the history and results of each time it ran by finding the original query in your search history.

API Keys & Authentication

⚠️ Before starting,** make sure you have reviewed the prerequisites. This guide explains how to obtain credentials to authenticate with the Read AI API programmatically. The Read AI API uses OAuth 2.1** with dynamic client registration. We support the Authorization Code grant with refresh tokens for obtaining and renewing access tokens. To call the API: 1. Register an OAuth 2.1 client using dynamic client registration. 2. Obtain an authorization code via the Authorization Code flow. 3. Exchange the authorization code for access and refresh tokens. 4. Use the access token to make API requests. 5. Refresh the access token as needed using the refresh token.

  • Tip: We recommend appending all curl requests with  | jq .  to get easier to visually parse outputs

Step 1: Register your OAuth client

This first step should be done from a command-line interface using e.g. curl. IMPORTANT: Save your client_id and client_secret immediately. The client_secret is sensitive and should be stored securely. ##### Example API Call

curl -X POST https://api.read.ai/oauth/register \ -H "Content-Type: application/json" \ -d '{ "client_name": "My Analytics Dashboard", "redirect_uris": ["https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui"], "grant_types": ["authorization_code", "refresh_token"], "response_types": ["code"], "scope": "openid email offline_access profile meeting:read mcp:execute", "token_endpoint_auth_method": "client_secret_basic" }'

You may specify any value you want for client_name but all other details should be left unmodified. ##### Example Response

{ "client_id": "test_id", "client_name": "My Analytics Dashboard", "client_secret": "super_secret", "redirect_uris": ["https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui"], "token_endpoint_auth_method": "client_secret_basic", "allowed_cors_origins": [], "grant_types": [ "authorization_code", "refresh_token" ], "scope": "openid email meeting:read offline_access profile mcp:execute", "audience": [ "https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings", "https://api.read.ai/mcp" ], "registration_client_uri": "https://authn.read.ai/oauth2/register", "registration_access_token": "access_token" }

Remember to save your client_id and client_secret, as these cannot be retrieved again later. #### Step 2: Obtain an authorization code This step happens in a web browser, from the Read AI OAuth page. ##### Initiate OAuth Flow Navigate to https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui and: - Input your client ID and client secret from your dynamic client registration. - The redirect URI should already be filled in correctly as https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui. - Click Start OAuth Flow when you are ready to proceed.

Sign In to Read AI

If you are not already logged in to Read AI in your browser, you will then get sent to the Read AI sign in page, where you must sign in with your account. While all of the SSO options should work, there have been some reports of users not getting automatically redirected back to the OAuth consent screen after logging in. If this happens, you can restart the process at https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui and it should skip the sign in step since you’re already authenticated. ##### Consent Screen Click Allow Access to consent to all of the scopes being granted to your access token. ##### Authorization Code Screen At this point, you have received your authorization code! In the next step, you’ll exchange this code for an access/refresh token pair. The most straightforward way to do this is to click Copy Command which you’ll then paste in your CLI. #### Step 3: Exchange authorization code for tokens ##### Return back to your command-line interface and run the command you copied at the end of the last step. Make sure to copy and securely save the access_token and refresh_token values. ##### Example Request

curl -X POST https://authn.read.ai/oauth2/token \ -H "Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded" \ -H "Authorization: Basic $(echo -n 'REDACTED' | base64)" \ -d "grant_type=authorization_code" \ -d "code=REDACTED" \ -d "redirect_uri=https://api.read.ai/oauth/ui" \ -d "code_verifier=REDACTED"
Example Response
{ "access_token": "REDACTED", "expires_in": 599, "id_token": "REDACTED", "refresh_token": "REDACTED", "scope": "openid profile email meeting:read mcp:execute offline_access", "token_type": "bearer" }

Step 4: Use the access token to make API calls

You can now use the access token to authenticate requests to the REST API by including it in the Authorization header as a Bearer token. ##### Example Request

curl -X GET "https://api.read.ai/oauth/test-token-with-scopes" \ -H "Authorization: Bearer ACCESS_TOKEN"

This is a simple endpoint for testing purposes. For a full listing of available API endpoints and other specification details, see the API Reference page. #### Step 5: Refresh the access token as needed Access tokens expire after 10 minutes and must be refreshed to continue making API requests. To obtain a new access token, send a request to the OAuth token endpoint using the refresh_token grant. Important: Refresh tokens are rotated on every use, with a short grace period to help account for concurrency. The token endpoint will return a new refresh token each time you exchange one. You must securely persist the new refresh token and discard the previous one. ##### Example Request

curl -X POST https://authn.read.ai/oauth2/token \ -H "Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded" \ -u "CLIENT_ID:CLIENT_SECRET" \ -d "grant_type=refresh_token" \ -d "refresh_token=YOUR_REFRESH_TOKEN"

API Reference

Read AI offers a public REST API, which is ideal for developers looking to build applications that programmatically access their Read AI meeting data. It is currently available as an open beta release. ⚠️ Before starting,** make sure you have reviewed the **prerequisites. - Authentication - Meeting States - Endpoints 1. List Meetings - 2. Retrieve a Meeting - 3. Retrieve a Live Meeting

Authentication

All endpoints require Bearer token authentication using the Authorization header. See API Keys & Authentication for instructions on how to obtain your access token. ##### Header Format

Authorization: Bearer YOUR_ACCESS_TOKEN
Example
curl https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings \ -H "Authorization: Bearer sk_test_123" \ -H "Accept: application/json"

For JWTs (used in OAuth2/Ory), Bearer would look like:

"Authorization: Bearer ory_at_..."

Meeting States

Meetings in the Read AI API can be in different states: - Active Meeting: A meeting currently in progress. Identified by end_time_ms being not present or null. - Ended Meeting: A completed meeting. Identified by end_time_ms having a value. - Live-Enabled Meeting: A meeting with real-time data capture enabled. Identified by live_enabled: true.

Endpoints

The base URL for all endpoints is https://api.read.ai/. ##### 1. List Meetings Method: GET /v1/meetings Description: Returns a paginated list of meetings in reverse chronological order (newest first), optionally filtered by start time. This endpoint returns both active and ended meetings, including their live-enabled status. Note that active meetings will have limited or no data for expandable fields, as these are generated after a meeting concludes. ###### Query Parameters - Name: Type: Description - limit: int: Number of results to return. Default 10, max 10. - start_time_ms.gt: int: Return meetings where start_time_ms is greater than this value. - start_time_ms.gte: int: Return meetings where start_time_ms is greater than or equal to this value. - start_time_ms.lt: int: Return meetings where start_time_ms is less than this value. - start_time_ms.lte: int: Return meetings where start_time_ms is less than or equal to this value. - cursor: str: Cursor for pagination; use the ID of the last object from the previous page. - expand[]: array: Fields to expand (see Expandable Fields section).

Example Request
curl "https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings?limit=5&start_time_ms.gte=1733700000000" \ -H "Authorization: Bearer sk_test_123" \ -H "Accept: application/json"
Example Response
{ "object": "list", "url": "/v1/meetings", "has_more": false, "data": [ { "id": "01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "start_time_ms": 1733800000000, "end_time_ms": 1733803600000, "scheduled_start_time_ms": 1733799800000, "scheduled_end_time_ms": 1733803400000, "participants": [ { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com", "invited": true, "attended": true } ], "owner": { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com" }, "title": "Weekly status sync", "report_url": "https://app.read.ai/analytics/meetings/01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "platform": "zoom", "platform_id": "987654321", "folders": ["Weekly Sync"], "live_enabled": false } ] }
2. Retrieve a Meeting

Method: GET /v1/meetings/{id} Description: Fetch a specific meeting by its unique ID. For active, live-enabled meetings, use the /v1/meetings/{id}/live endpoint instead. ###### Path Parameters - Name: Type: Description - id: string: Meeting ULID

Query Parameters
  • Name: Type: Description
  • expand[]: array: Fields to expand in the response
Example Request
curl "https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings/01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP?expand[]=summary&expand[]=metrics" \ -H "Authorization: Bearer sk_test_123" \ -H "Accept: application/json"
Example Response
{ "id": "01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "start_time_ms": 1733800000000, "end_time_ms": 1733803600000, "scheduled_start_time_ms": 1733799800000, "scheduled_end_time_ms": 1733803400000, "participants": [ { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com", "invited": true, "attended": true } ], "owner": { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com" }, "title": "Weekly status sync", "report_url": "https://app.read.ai/analytics/meetings/01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "platform": "zoom", "platform_id": "987654321", "folders": ["Weekly Sync"], "live_enabled": false, "summary": "We reviewed project timelines and assigned new action items.", "metrics": { "read_score": 0.9, "sentiment": 0.3, "engagement": 0.75 } }
3. Retrieve a Live Meeting

Method: GET /v1/meetings/{id}/live Description: Retrieve live meeting data for an ongoing meeting by its unique ID. This endpoint allows you to fetch real-time transcript and chapter summaries as the meeting progresses, with optional filtering by start time. Important Note: All meetings do not have live data by default, it is only available if you have the live dashboard open during the meeting. We will be adding an account setting to enable live data for all meetings as part of the full GA release. ###### Path Parameters - Name: Type: Description - id: string: Meeting** **ULID

Query Parameters
  • Name: Type: Description
  • Name: Type: Description
  • start_time_ms.gt: int: Return live data where start_time_ms is greater than this value
  • start_time_ms.gte: int: Return live data where start_time_ms is greater than or equal to this value
  • expand[]: array: Fields to expand in the response (only transcript and chapter_summaries are available for live meetings)
Example Request
curl "https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings/01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP/live?expand[]=transcript&start_time_ms.gte=1733800000000" \ -H "Authorization: Bearer sk_test_123" \ -H "Accept: application/json"
Example Response
{ "id": "01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "start_time_ms": 1733800000000, "end_time_ms": null, "scheduled_start_time_ms": 1733799800000, "scheduled_end_time_ms": 1733803400000, "participants": [ { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com", "invited": true, "attended": true } ], "owner": { "name": "Alice Example", "email": "alice@example.com" }, "title": "Weekly status sync", "report_url": "https://app.read.ai/analytics/meetings/01HFYH0A6JM4R7MZ2E6X5T9BNP", "platform": "zoom", "platform_id": "987654321", "folders": ["Weekly Sync"], "live_enabled": true, "transcript": { "speakers": [ { "name": "Alice Example" } ], "turns": [ { "speaker": { "name": "Alice Example" }, "text": "Let's start with the project updates.", "start_time_ms": 1733800000000, "end_time_ms": 1733800005000 } ], "text": "[Alice Example]: Let's start with the project updates." } }

Expandable Fields

The API supports the expand[] parameter to fetch additional enriched fields that are not included by default. Note that using expanded fields will increase response times as the API retrieves and processes additional data. Expanding multiple fields or using expansions on large list requests may result in noticeably slower processing times. ##### Available Fields - summary - chapter_summaries - action_items - key_questions - topics - transcript - metrics - recording_download

Example Request
curl "https://api.read.ai/v1/meetings?expand[]=metrics&expand[]=transcript" \ -H "Authorization: Bearer sk_test_123" \ -H "Accept: application/json"

Pagination

The API uses cursor-based pagination for navigating through large result sets. ##### Parameters - Parameter: Description - limit: Maximum number of objects to return. Default 10, max 10. - cursor: Cursor pointing to the next page. Use the ID of the last object from the prior response.

Response Fields
  • Field: Type: Description
  • object: str: Typically “list”.
  • url: str: URL of the request.
  • has_more: bool: Indicates whether additional pages exist.
  • data: array: List of returned meeting objects.
Example Usage

The cursor parameter should be set to the ID of the last** meeting returned in the previous page’s data array. ###### Step 1: First Page Request Method:** GET /v1/meetings Example Response:

{ "object": "list", "url": "/v1/meetings", "has_more": true, "data": [ { "id": "01KG7BZV04HJGVBM4KJCWV9P4N", "title": "Team Standup", ... }, { "id": "01KG76TMYXDRZ0D86Z3MNXP566", "title": "Client Call", ... }, ... { "id": "01KG4JRNA0EEFAJTM0S3PMDARK", // ← Last meeting id in this page "title": "Sprint Planning", ... } ] }
Step 2: Second Page Request

Take the id from the last object in the data array (01KG4JRNA0EEFAJTM0S3PMDARK) and use it as the cursor: Method: GET /v1/meetings?cursor=01KG4JRNA0EEFAJTM0S3PMDARK This will return the next page of results starting after that meeting. ###### Step 3: Continue Until Done Keep repeating with the new last ID until has_more is false. #### Error Codes Common error responses follow standard HTTP status codes. - Status Code: Meaning: Description - 200: OK: Request succeeded. - 400: Bad Request: Your request parameters were malformed or invalid. - 401: Unauthorized: Authentication failed or missing token. - 403: Forbidden: You do not have permission to access this resource. - 404: Not Found: The requested resource does not exist. - 422: Unprocessable Entity: One or more parameters failed validation. - 429: Too Many Requests: Rate limit exceeded; retry later. - 500: Server Error: Error occurred on the server.

Applying report templates

Applying a template

You can apply a report template to structure a meeting report around a specific meeting type, framework, or workflow. 1. Open a meeting report. 2. Open the Templates dropdown. 3. Select a template.

The template is first applied to your personal view only. Nothing changes for anyone else. If you ever want to return to the original default template, Read AI Recap, you can always available in the template picker #### Personal vs. Shared views Every meeting report has two views: - Personal view: Visible only to you. This view is updated first when you apply a template. - Shared view: Visible to everyone with access to the report. This view changes only when an owner or editor subsequently publishes a personal view.

You can try different templates in your personal view without affecting teammates or external viewers, such as customers or prospects. #### Publish a template to the Shared view When you’re ready to make your template and report layout visible to everyone: 1. Open the Templates dropdown. 2. Select Set as Shared View. 3. Confirm that you want to update the shared view.

You’ll also be prompted to set the template as the shared view immediately after applying it. You can confirm the prompt or dismiss it and publish your view later. Only meeting report owners and editors can update the shared view. #### Additional considerations - View indicator: The meeting report indicates whether you’re viewing your personal view or the shared view. You can switch between them at any time. - Editing a template: Changes to a template affect everyone who uses that template. Read AI displays a reminder before you save your changes. - Pushing a report to a CRM: When you push a meeting report to a connected CRM, Read AI sends your personal view. This lets you maintain an external-friendly shared view while sending your preferred report format to the CRM.

Ask Read in a folder

Ask Read now works across any folder, surfacing insights, decisions, and action items from a specific set of reports. Users can run Ask Read queries scoped to a specific folder automatically while on the individual folder page. Users can also configure the queries by the type of search, date range, sources or access type when running the queries in this folder. Users can also change the scope from “Search this folder” to “Search everything” by clicking the folder icon.

Automatically push templated reports to your CRM

Read AI can automatically push meeting reports to HubSpot or Salesforce using a selected report template. This helps standardize the meeting insights sent to your CRM and keeps customer records consistent across your workspace. CRM auto-push with report templates is currently supported only for HubSpot and Salesforce. #### Configure CRM auto-push with a template To select the template used for reports automatically pushed to your CRM: 1. Go to your Integration settings. If you’re a workspace admin configuring this for your organization, go to Workspace Integration settings. 2. Find HubSpot or Salesforce. 3. Enable Auto-Push Meeting Notes. 4. Open the Push Template dropdown and select the template you want to use.

The selected template is applied to all meeting reports automatically pushed to the CRM from your workspace. Note: The Push Template setting controls how reports are formatted when sent to the CRM. It does not change anyone’s personal or shared view of a meeting report in Read AI. #### Change the CRM push template To use a different template, return to your personal or workspace Integration settings and select another option from the Push Template dropdown. Future reports pushed automatically to the CRM will use the newly selected template. Changing this setting does not update reports that were previously pushed to the CRM.

Basic folder actions

This article guides you through the essential actions for managing folders in Read AI: creating new folders, deleting folders you no longer need, and editing folder details to keep your organization up to date. #### Creating a New Folder ##### Where to Create a New Folder Users can create a folder from the Folders page, the Reports page, the main side navigation and the Folders Settings page. 1. From the Folders page: Click the “New Folder” button on the Folders page to create a folder.

  1. From the Reports page: Use the folder management options in the folders column dropdown to create a new folder directly from your report list.

  2. Side Navigation: Click the “+” option in the main side navigation to quickly create a new folder from anywhere in the app.

  3. From the Folders Settings page: Go to “Account Settings” > “Folders” and click “Add New Folder” to create and manage folders from your settings.

New Folder Options

When you create a new folder, a window will appear allowing you to enter the following details: - Enter the following details:

Folder Type (required): - Name (required): Enter a unique name for your folder. - Icon (optional): Choose an icon for your folder. If none is selected, a default icon will be used. - Color (optional): Pick a color for your folder. If not selected, a default color will be assigned automatically. - Folder Description (optional): Add a description to help identify the folder’s purpose. - Complete the required fields and any optional details, then save to create your new folder.

(image: Screenshot 2025-12-23 at 6.33.50 PM.png)(image: Screenshot 2025-12-23 at 6.35.34 PM.png) #### Deleting a Folder Deleting a folder only removes the folder organization—your reports are not deleted and remain accessible elsewhere. Deleting a personal custom and shared folders is a permanent action, and you will be prompted with a confirmation dialog before the folder is removed. For personal Smart Folders, deleting simply hides the folder from view. You will see a confirmation dialog, and can unhide the folder at any time from the Settings page. You can delete a folder from the folder list page, the individual folder page, or the folders settings page. 1. From the Folders page: Click the “more options menu”, then “Delete Folder”.

  1. From the individual folder page: Click the “three dot menu” then “Delete Folder”.

  2. From the Folder Settings page: You can delete custom folders by selecting the “x”, and you can hide Smart Folders by selecting the “eye” icon.

Editing a Folder

You can update your folder’s information and appearance as your needs change. When editing, you can modify the following details: - Folder Type - Name (not for Smart Folders) - Icon - Color - Folder Description (not for Smart Folders)

You can edit a folder from both the folder list page and the individual folder page. 1. From the Folders page: Click the “three dot menu”, then “Edit Folder”.

  1. From the individual folder page: Click the “three dot menu” then “Edit Folder”.

Can I use multiple calendars with the Read Smart Scheduler links?

Currently, the Read Smart Scheduler links are only able to use one of your calendars (both for scheduling meetings on and for determining your availability). Read will use whichever one is set as the “Scheduling Calendar” under Account Settings > Smart Scheduler.

Can Read detect if the meeting screen is minimized or covered by another window?

Read does not measure active window, rather we limit ourselves intentionally to features (audio, video, screenshare) that are available as a participant in the video conference.

Connecting Read AI to Microsoft Teams

Integrating Read AI with Microsoft Teams unlocks powerful productivity features for your meetings and daily workflow. With the Teams integration, you can automatically receive meeting summaries, daily recaps of your chats and channels, and more, directly in your Teams application. By syncing your Teams content to Read AI, you also enable Readout topic reports and Ask Read, empowering your queries with all the knowledge from your Teams chats and channels. This integration is available to all Read users with a work or school Microsoft account, including those on the Free plan. #### Prerequisites - Microsoft 365 or Teams Account: To use all features of the integration, you must use a work or school (not personal) Microsoft account. Personal Microsoft accounts (e.g., @outlook.com, @hotmail.com) can be connected but functionality will be limited. - Read AI Account: Sign up for Read AI using Microsoft SSO or connect your Microsoft account after registration. - Permissions: Some features may require approval by a Teams admin, especially for channel access

How to Connect to Microsoft Teams

You can connect your Read AI account to Microsoft Teams during the account creation process, or at any point later on from your Integrations page: - Locate the Microsoft Teams integration and click Connect. - Follow the prompts to sign in with your Microsoft credentials and grant the necessary permissions. You may see after connecting that admin approval is required to enable full functionality - specifically to grant access to your Teams channels. - If so, Read AI will generate a link that you can give to an admin to approve (they do not need to have a Read AI account). - Once approved, you’ll have to confirm this from your Teams integration page to complete setup. - Going forward, other users in your Teams tenant will be able to fully connect without going through this step.

You can also connect by installing the Read AI app in Microsoft Teams: - Open Microsoft Teams and go to the Apps section. - Search for “Read AI” or use the direct AppSource link. - Click Get It Now and follow the prompts to add the app to Teams. - Sign in with your Read AI account if prompted. - Tip: You can pin the Read AI app to your Teams navigation for quick access. - After connecting, you should still visit your Teams integration page to configure your channels and other settings.

For more details on installing or using the Read AI app in Teams, see How to install Read’s app for Teams. #### Enabling and Configuring Teams Features There are multiple different features available through the Teams integration, most of which can be configured from your Integrations page: - Daily Summaries: Choose which Teams channels receive daily recaps of activity. You can opt to receive these as direct messages and/or sent to the channels that are being summarized. See Teams Daily Summaries for chat and channels for more details. - Meeting Reports: Automatically receive Read AI meeting summaries in Teams after your meetings conclude, and/or get Pre-Reads delivered before your next recurring meeting. See this support article for more details. - Live Meeting Dashboard: With the Read AI app for Teams, access real-time transcripts, summaries, and metrics during Teams meetings via the Read AI app sidebar. See Using Read’s live meeting dashboard for more details. - Ask Read: As long as your “Incorporate Teams Data into Read” setting is enabled, your Teams chat and channel messages will be used to help power Ask Read, allowing you to search and ask questions about your data with the power of AI. See this support article for more details.

Configure which notifications and reports you receive, and adjust other preferences at any time from the Integrations page. #### Workspace Admin Controls Read AI workspace admins have additional controls to manage how Teams integration features are used across the organization. From the workspace settings page in the “Integrations” section, admins can enable or disable the ability for users to push content to Teams, to sync Teams messages to Read AI, or to connect the Teams integration at all: #### Using Read AI in Teams Meetings There are multiple ways to add Read AI to your Teams meetings: - The easiest way is to connect your calendar (e.g. Outlook or Google Calendar), allowing you to configure which meetings Read AI should join automatically. - You can also manually add Read AI to a Teams meeting in progress, which is especially useful for last minute or ad hoc meetings. Note that this option doesn’t allow reports to be automatically shared with participants or auto-synced with HubSpot or Salesforce. - Whether Read AI auto-joins or is manually added, you can use the Read AI app for Teams to access the live meeting dashboard, with real-time transcripts, summaries, metrics, and other tools for managing your meeting reports.

After your meetings, summaries and action items are automatically posted to your selected Teams locations and available in your Read AI dashboard. #### Troubleshooting & FAQs - Common Issues: If you encounter errors connecting Teams, ensure you are using a work or school account and that your organization has not restricted third-party app access. Admin approval may be required for some permissions. - Disconnecting Teams: To remove the integration, return to the Integrations page and click Disconnect next to Microsoft Teams. - Support: For persistent issues, please reach out to Read AI support.

Connecting Read AI to Slack

You can connect Read AI and Slack in order to send your meeting notes to Slack and unlock other powerful features to boost productivity. This page provides an overview of these features, with links out to other pages for more details. These features are available to all Read AI users, including those on the free plan. Read’s integration with Slack has two distinct options, which can both be enabled by connecting to Slack on the Integrations page. (image: Screenshot 2025-03-24 at 11.35.55 AM.png) #### Push to Slack The Push to Slack integration allows you to send your meeting notes and other content from Read AI to Slack. To enable, go to your Integrations page, find the Push to Slack entry and click Connect. For more detailed instructions, see: - Integrations | Enabling Push to Slack for your account

Automatic

Once connected, you’ll see a few options to customize the content that will automatically get sent to Slack: There are four different types of messages that can be turned on or off entirely from this page, with Manage links to further configure how these messages are sent: - Meeting Reports can be sent to one of three destination options via the Manage link: “Only me” — Read AI will send you a DM - “People with access” — Read AI will send individual DMs to anyone who gets access to the report (which is typically people who were invited to the meeting). Note that this will only work for people who are in your Slack workspace. - “Send to a channel” — Read AI will send all your notes to a specific channel of your choosing. You can only send to private channels that the Read AI bot/app has been added to.

Manual

In addition to the automatic sending, you can also manually send meeting notes and topic readouts to Slack. Just look for the Slack icon or a “Send via…” button in the top right corner of the report page. For more details, see: - Integrations | How can I share meeting reports to Slack?

Slack Sync to Read

The Slack Sync to Read integration allows users to search their Slack messages with Ask Read. To enable, go to your Integrations page, find the Slack Sync to Read entry and click Connect. This will only give Read AI access to the messages that you have access to in Slack. Read AI uses Slack’s MCP server to search all of your messages in real time, without actually saving the messages to Read AI. Once connected, you can search and ask questions about your Slack messages from the Ask Read page or the various search buttons that appear throughout Read’s web, desktop, and mobile apps. You can also use the filters to restrict your sources to just Slack, or even specific Slack channels: (image: Screenshot 2025-04-03 at 7.15.59 AM.png) If you’ve connected Push to Slack, you can also Ask Read questions directly from the Read AI app in Slack. This gives you convenient access to a powerful, universal search engine and question answering bot that doesn’t only reference your Slack messages, it also includes all of your meetings as well as other connected sources. #### The Read AI app Once you or anyone else in your Slack workspace has connected the Push to Slack integration, you’ll be able to find the Read AI app in Slack. If you haven’t connected Slack to your own Read AI account yet, you’ll be prompted to do so when you go to the app, and once you have you’ll see something like this: The Home tab has a few key features: - See any In Progress meetings that are currently being recorded by Read AI. If available, you’ll find a button to open the live dashboard in your browser, giving you access live meeting transcription, metrics, and other tools. - You can also find your Recent Meetings for easy access to view and share your meeting reports. - At the bottom is a list of Upcoming Events, with convenient links to join the meeting or to “add Read” so that it will get recorded.

The Chat tab is where you go to ask questions of Ask Read, and the History tab is where you can see all of the previous messages Read AI sent you.

Connecting your Read account to Confluence

With Read’s Confluence integration, you can push meeting reports directly to Confluence. For organizations that like to keep notes in Confluence, this is a great way to ensure your Read meeting notes are shared in a centralized location and searchable alongside all of your other notes and documentation. Confluence is one of Read’s premium integrations, meaning it’s only available to users on the Pro, Enterprise, or Enterprise+ plans. #### Connecting the integration Like all of Read’s integrations, you can find and enable the Confluence integration from your Integrations page: After clicking the “Connect” button, you’ll get sent to Atlassian to authorize the connection. If you belong to a Read workspace, you may need to ask a workspace admin to enable the integration first before you can connect. They can do this by going to Manage Workspace > Settings > Integrations and toggling on Confluence. Once you click “Connect”, you’ll see a screen that looks similar to this, which shows the different permissions Read requires to enable the integration. Click on “Accept” to proceed: #### (image: Screenshot 2024-10-21 at 10.14.42 PM.png) You should get redirected back to the Integrations page, where Confluence will now appear as “Active”. You can now choose the default format for your page titles when saving notes to Confluence, whether or not you’d like to automatically push meeting notes to Confluence, and whether or not you’d like to make Confluence content available with Ask Read: #### Manually Pushing Meeting Notes to Confluence If you opt to not auto-save meeting notes in Confluence, manually push notes there is as easy as clicking on “Push to…” in the top-right corner of a meeting report, and then selecting Confluence: This will open up a dialog, where you must choose the space, and optionally a parent page you want to create your meeting notes page under. If you don’t select a parent page, the notes will be written directly in the root of your space. You can also choose to edit the title and whether or not to include the full transcript: #### (image: Screenshot 2024-10-21 at 10.21.18 PM.png) Please be aware that Confluence doesn’t allow two pages in the same space to have the same title, so trying to push a page with a duplicate title will give you a warning and let you opt to overwrite the existing page. One nice feature of this integration is that it will remember the space and parent page you chose last time, saving you time if you tend to push your notes to the same location. After you click “Create”, assuming no errors, the page will be created immediately and you’ll see a green notification in the bottom right of your screen with a link to view the page on Confluence: #### (image: Screenshot 2024-10-21 at 10.32.11 PM.png) - Connecting your Read account to Jira

Connecting your Read account to Google Drive

When you connect your Read account to Google Drive, you’ll be able to use Ask Read to query and write prompts for any documents you store in Google Drive! - Supported document types include: Google Docs, Google Sheets, Google Slides and PDFs. Images contained within these documents are not included. - Updates made to files will automatically be synced with Read and available on Ask Read after a brief processing delay. - Read will have access to any files you have access to within Google Drive– there is not currently a way to selectively sync files. - All files are included in Ask Read, regardless of age.

Setting up the connection

Like all Read integrations, you can get started by logging into your account and visiting the ‘Integration’ tab, or by clicking here to go directly to the Google Drive integration. 1. Start by clicking on ‘Connect’ in the Drive integration window (image: Screenshot 2025-03-24 at 4.52.31 PM.png) 2. You’ll be prompted to login to your Google account. After selecting your account, you’ll be asked to grant Read permission to view files in your Drive 3. When you’re ready, click ‘Allow’ to be redirected back to the Read integrations page. 4. You’re done! After a short delay, your Google Drive data will be available for reference in Ask Read.

Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot

With Read AI for HubSpot, you’re getting a CRM Copilot that supercharges your HubSpot experience in multiple ways: - Read’s Meeting Assistant will handle the note taking during your sales calls, letting you fully engage with your prospects when it matters most. Read syncs meeting notes, action items, and transcripts directly to your Contacts, Companies and Deals in HubSpot - Read’s Ask Readis a powerful enterprise search tool that can answer questions and generate complex responses using the information in your HubSpot account, in addition to past meeting transcripts and other sources like email, chat, and documentation platforms. - Read will also analyze yourAsk Read Salesforce opportunities and make recommendations for when they are ready to move to a new stage.

Read AI is the perfect tool to help you catch up on prospect progress ahead of your calls by reviewing past notes, get answers to questions potentially in real time during your calls, and keep your HubSpot up to date as your opportunities evolve. #### Prerequisites - The HubSpot integration is only available on Pro, Enterprise, and Enterprise+ plans. - A HubSpot Super Admin** or a user with ****“App Marketplace Access” permission** will need to be the first Read AI user in your workspace to connect an account before subsequent connections are allowed - Consider whether you want to connect HubSpot for your entire workspace instead of just your own account, see Connecting your Read Workspace to HubSpot

Getting Started

  1. Visit the HubSpot Integration Page in your Read AI account and click “Connect”
  2. Enter your HubSpot credentials of your primary HubSpot account when prompted
  3. Review the requested app permissions– when you’re ready to continue, click “Connect App”
  4. You’re done!
Integration Settings & Options

Once you’ve connected Read AI to your HubSpot account, you’ll be able to select several configuration options: - Auto-Push Meeting Notes to HubSpot: The core functionality of the integration, this enables Read AI to sync meeting notes to your Contacts and associated Deals in HubSpot. - Incorporate HubSpot data into Read: This allows us to include your HubSpot data for Ask Read, and also to provide recommendations for your deal stage updates. - Create new HubSpot Contacts: Enabling this will cause new Contacts to be created if no corresponding contact is found in your HubSpot instance. - Include Meeting Transcript: When this is enabled, the full transcript from your meeting report will be attached to the Meeting Activity as a file. When it’s disabled, only summary report data will be included. - Sync past meetings: When clicked, this will backfill all previous meeting reports that included external participants into HubSpot.Note that this option is currently unavailable if you have HubSpot connected via a workspace-level HubSpot integration

Locating Synced Meeting Reports in HubSpot

With auto-push, each meeting report is synced to the Contact record(s) in HubSpot (for external participants only), and is also viewable on any Deals associated with the Contact(s). Using the manual “Push to HubSpot” option from within a report, you can also sync meeting notes to Company records. You can find the meeting report in the “Activity” tab of a Contact, Company or Deal, specifically under the “Meetings” sub-tab: ##### Important notes - Only external participants (that is, participants with an email address in another domain than yours) are eligible to have meeting reports synced to their HubSpot record. - If a Contact does not exist as a record in HubSpot at the time of the meeting, and you have “Create new HubSpot contacts” option in your Read account disabled, then the meeting report will not sync to that participant. - Meeting reports will also be viewable on any Deals that Contact is associated with. - Meetings where Read is manually added to the meeting (pasting the meeting URL into “Add to Live Meeting” on the Read website/inviting the assistant via Read’s apps/inviting Read via the Chrome extension) won’t automatically sync because there is no calendar invite. - Meeting notes will only sync to HubSpot automatically if you have Owner or Editor access to the report when the meeting report is first generated - Users will need to have “Delete” permissions on Companies, Contacts, Deals, and Files in order to connect the integration. If you see the error message below, these are likely the missing permissions that need to be adjusted. (image: Screenshot 2025-03-04 at 21.36.43.png)

Connecting your Read account to Jira

With Read’s Jira integration, you can easily create new Jira issues straight from your meeting action items on Read. For anyone who uses Jira, this is a great way to help you keep track of and follow up on long-running tasks that could otherwise get buried in your Read meeting reports. Jira is one of Read’s premium integrations, meaning it’s only available to users on the Pro, Enterprise, or Enterprise+ plans. #### Connecting the integration Like all other integrations, you can find and enable the connection with Jira from your Integrations page: Simply click on the “Connect” button and you’ll be sent to Atlassian to authorize. If you belong to a Read workspace, you may need to ask a workspace admin to enable the integration first before you can connect. They can do this by going to Manage Workspace > Settings > Integrations and toggling on Jira. After clicking “Connect” on the Integrations page, you should see a screen like this listing the permissions Read requires for the integration to work, with an “Accept” button on the bottom - click that button: And that’s it - you should now see the listing show as “Active” with a disconnect button your Integrations page. #### Using the integration Once connected, you can start using the integration right away. Just go to your meeting report, hover over an action item, and click the Jira icon that shows up in the top right corner: (image: Screenshot 2024-10-21 at 4.17.52 PM.png) A dialog will then pop up, allowing you to fill in more details that will get written to the ticket. The action item text will be pre-filled in the Description, but you can edit it before submitting: Note that Read will pull these fields dynamically from your Jira account, depending on the selected project and issue type, so yours will likely look slightly different from this screenshot. Also pay attention to any fields that are not marked as “optional”, as you will need to specify some value for these in order to click “Create”. One nice feature of this integration is that Read will remember your previous selection for things like project and issue type, making it easier to create subsequent issues. After clicking “Create”, the issue will be created, and you’ll see a notification in the bottom right of the screen with a link to view your new ticket on Jira: (image: Screenshot 2024-10-21 at 4.29.29 PM.png) The description in Jira will also automatically include a link back to the Read meeting report that it was created from. - Connecting your Read account to Confluence
