Be aware that if you added Read manually, your title won’t get pulled from any calendar event and may be something generic like “Teams Meeting”. ##### Contents of the meeting reports Each document will look something like this: Within it, you’ll find a few sections: - Metadata at the top (meeting title, link to read.ai, event time, platform, and participants) - AI-generated summary, with chapters & topics breakdown - Action items - Key questions - Full transcript

Lastly, remember that if you want to adjust the sharing on this document, just click on the “Share” button at the top right of the Google Docs interface. #### What other platforms can I push meeting reports to? Read supports automated push to: - Google Docs (Current article) - Microsoft OneNote

How do I get live notes?

The Google Docs integration previously supported a live notes feature, which was deprecated in February 2026. If you still want the live summaries or transcript, you can use the live meeting dashboard. There you can view live notes, as well as real-time metrics and access other tools during your meeting.

Saving Meeting Notes to Microsoft OneNote

  • Enabling the Microsoft OneNote integration

  • How it works Finding your meeting reports on Microsoft OneNote

  • Contents of the meeting reports

  • February 2 2026 Live Notes with Microsoft OneNote feature had been deprecated. If you rely on real-time visibility, you can continue using the Read AI live dashboard. Read AI no longer streams live transcription and summaries to Google Docs or OneNote. Instead, your full meeting report will be automatically saved to your connected Google Docs or Microsoft OneNote account after the meeting ends, once processing is complete.

There are a few ways to automate pushing meeting reports with Read AI - this article will explain how to push meeting reports to Microsoft OneNote, but you can see below for other methods. #### Enabling the Microsoft OneNote integration Automatically saving your meeting notes to OneNote is easy, simply locate Microsoft OneNote on your Integrations page and click Connect: After going through the authorization with Microsoft to grant the necessary permission, you’ll get sent back to the Integrations page. The specific permissions required for the OneNote integration are: - OPENID - EMAIL - PROFILE - USER_READ - ONENOTE_READ_WRITE - ONEDRIVE_READ_WRITE

In order for this to automatically push meeting notes to your Microsoft OneNote, you must have the Auto-Save Meeting Notes in OneNote setting turned on: By default, these notesbooks are only visible to yourself, but you can always adjust the sharing directly from OneNote after they’re created. #### How it works ##### Finding your meeting reports on Microsoft OneNote All meeting reports are written to the default location for new Microsoft OneNote notebooks on your Microsoft OneDrive in a notebook titled Read AI Meeting Notes.  Please do not rename or move this notebook, otherwise Read will generate a new one in the same location after the next meeting. https://onedrive.live.com/login Inside this notebook, there will be a section called Meeting Notes which contains pages for all of your notes. Each page corresponds to a single meeting report, and are named using the date and time of the meeting (in YYYY-MM-DD), followed by the meeting title. Be aware that if you added Read manually, your title won’t get pulled from any calendar event and may be something generic like “Teams Meeting”. #### Contents of the meeting reports Each page will look something like this: Within it, you’ll find a few sections: - Metadata at the top (meeting title, link to read.ai, event time, platform, and participants) - AI-generated summary, with chapters & topics breakdown - Action items - Key questions - Full transcript

Lastly, remember that if you want to adjust the sharing on this document, just click on the “Share” button at the top right of the Microsoft OneNote interface. #### My meeting notes did not push to OneNote, what happened? Please note that Read will not push incomplete meeting notes to Microsoft OneNote, if the meeting notes are showing up under your Read AI dashboard > Reports > Incomplete, these will not be pushed. You also need to have direct access to the meeting notes. Your meeting note will not push if you only have access to the that meeting note through: - Will not pushed: workspace’s Teams Report Access or Global Access. - Will not pushed: manually sharing by the report owner after the meeting ends.

Currently, there is no way to manually push meeting notes to Microsoft OneNote if the automatic push failed. #### What other platforms can I push meeting reports to? Read supports automated push to: - Microsoft OneNote (Current article) - Google Docs

How do I get live notes?

The Microsoft OneNote integration previously supported a live notes feature, which was deprecated in February 2026. If you still want the live summaries or transcript, you can use the live meeting dashboard. There you can view live notes, as well as real-time metrics and access other tools during your meeting.

Searching Your HubSpot Content using Ask Read

Overview

Ask Read is more than just a search tool; it’s a generative AI assistant that lets you query your entire knowledge base, including HubSpot, emails, meetings, documents, and more. This means you can ask complex, cross-platform questions and get synthesized, actionable answers, complete with citations and links to the original sources. While Ask Read is adept at surfacing insights that span multiple data types and systems, it is also great for just finding that single record in HubSpot or other piece of information that you don’t want to waste time tracking down. #### Prerequisites - A Read account with Ask Read enabled. - HubSpot integration connected and authorized in Read (requires a Read Pro/Enterprise/Enterprise+ plan) - Appropriate permissions in HubSpot for the data you want to search.

Step 1: Connect Your HubSpot Account

  1. Log in to Read.
  2. Connect Your Read Account to HubSpot Depending on your situation, you may need a HubSpot Super Admin to help with this
  3. Read workspace admins also have the option of connecting HubSpot for everyone in your workspace

Step 2: Run Powerful Searches Across HubSpot and More

  • Open Ask Read in Read.
  • Enter your query using natural language. For example, you can: Ask for summaries that combine HubSpot deals, meeting notes, and emails (e.g., “Summarize all recent activity for the Acme Corp deal, including meetings, emails, and HubSpot notes”).
  • Prepare for meetings by aggregating CRM, email, and document insights (e.g., “What open action items and recent communications do I have for contacts at Acme Corp?”).
  • Draft follow-up emails using context from meetings and HubSpot records (e.g., “Draft a follow-up email to Jane Smith at Acme Corp referencing our last meeting and current deal stage”).
  • Identify pipeline risks by combining deal data and meeting sentiment (e.g., “Which deals in HubSpot are at risk based on negative meeting sentiment or lack of recent activity?”).

Step 3: Refine and Explore Further

  • Continue the conversation: Ask follow-up questions or clarify your request in the chat interface to drill deeper or adjust the scope.
  • Run separate queries for different perspectives or to see all source citations and related content.
  • Use filters for date, source, or record type if you want to narrow your results up front, but conversational refinement is often more powerful.

(Optional) Step 4: Use the HubSpot UI extension for convenient access

  • The Read integration with HubSpot also includes custom app cards that you can use to submit Ask Read queries directly from your HubSpot UI.
  • See this article for more details on how to set this up: Using Read’s HubSpot UI Extension

Troubleshooting

  • If HubSpot results are missing, confirm your integration is connected and the initial sync completed successfully (check your email for a notification).
  • If results appear outdated, you may need to rerun your query later after Read syncs the latest updates.
  • Read can only connect to a single HubSpot account, if you have multiple, make sure that you selected the right one when first connecting.
  • Some HubSpot record types are not currently searchable, including tickets, files, tasks, and custom objects.

Additional Resources

  • Using Ask Read to search your meetings and connected apps
  • Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot
  • Using Read’s HubSpot UI Extension

Shared folders

Using Shared Folders makes it easy to collaborate with colleagues, teams, or entire workspaces by providing a controlled way to organize and share collections of meeting reports. They streamline access management, support dynamic teams, and help ensure everyone has the context they need. #### What is a Shared Folder? A Shared Folder is a collection of meeting reports that is accessible to multiple users. When you create a shared folder, you can invite individuals, teams, or workspaces as members. All members receive access to every report in the folder at the permission level you specify (Viewer or Editor). The key difference between a Personal Folder and a Shared Folder, is that you can only add reports to a Shared Folder that you are already an Owner or Editor of. You cannot add reports to a Shared Folder if you are only a Viewer. This is because you need sufficient permissions to be able to change the sharing distribution of a report. #### How to Create a Shared Folder 1. Create a Folder: You can create a new folder from the Folders page, Reports page, side navigation or Folder Settings page (learn more). Select “Create Folder” from any of those options. 2. Choose Folder Type: By default, folders are personal. You must explicitly choose “Shared Folder” as the folder type. 3. Set Folder Details: Just like a personal folder, you can select a name, icon, color and description. 4. Choose Report Access: Choose whether members will have “Viewer” or “Editor” access to the reports in this folder (see below for details). When in doubt, we recommend choosing the lowest level of permission, “Viewer”. 5. Add Folder Members: Invite individuals, teams, or entire workspaces to the folder. 6. Choose Folder Permission Level: Not only do users have a relationship to the reports in the folder (“Report Access”), but they also have a permission relative to the Folder. You must choose if these user are Editors or Viewers of the folder. 7. Add Reports: Select reports to include in the folder. Only reports you own or have existing Owner or Editor rights to can be added to a shared folder. 8. Create Folder: Click “Create” to finalize. All invited members will immediately have access to the folder and its contents.

What are “Report Access” Permissions?

When you set up a shared folder, you assign a default access level for all folder members using the “Report Access” setting: - Viewer: Members can view all reports in the folder but cannot edit or remove them. - Editor: Members can view, edit, and remove reports from the folder.

The access level applies to every report in the folder for all members. If a member already has higher access to a report without the folder (e.g., direct editor access), they retain the highest level of access. For example, if a user already has Editor access to a report, and then they are added to a Shared Folder that contains the report and has Report Access set to Viewer, that user will retain their original Editor access. #### What are Folder Permissions? Folder members are the users, teams, or workspaces you invite to a shared folder. Each member is assigned a role that determines what they can do specifically to the folder: - Owner: The user who created the folder. Owners can manage all aspects of the folder, including membership, settings, and converting between personal and shared folders. There is only one owner per folder, but you can transfer your own ownership to someone else. - Collaborator: Collaborators can add or remove reports, manage membership (add/remove collaborators and members), and update folder settings. - Member: Members can see all reports in the folder but cannot add, remove, or manage reports or members (they can, however, remove reports that they own from the folder). Members can see who the folder owner is, and the count of total members, but cannot see who the other members are.

You can update member roles at any time from the edit folder page. Only the owner can transfer ownership or convert the folder type. #### Managing Shared Folders - Adding or Removing Members: Folder owners and collaborators can add or remove members at any time. New members immediately receive access to all reports in the folder. - Changing Report Access Levels: Folder owners and collaborators can edit the “Report Access” setting to change whether this folder will grant members editor or viewer access to the contained reports. - Changing Members’ Roles: Folder owners and collaborators can change a member’s role (collaborator/member) as needed. Collaborators can’t change the owner’s role, only the owner can make someone else the owner of their folder. - Removing Reports: Only owners and collaborators can remove reports from a shared folder (except viewers can remove reports that they own). Removing a report does not delete it; it simply removes it from the shared context. - Leaving a Folder: Any member can leave a shared folder at any time. Reports they added remain in the folder unless removed by another editor or the owner.

Best Practices

  • Use shared folders for project teams, onboarding, or recurring cross-functional meetings to keep everyone aligned.
  • Configure Report Access setting and members’ roles carefully to ensure only trusted users can modify folder contents and membership.
  • Regularly review folder membership to maintain appropriate access.

Teams Daily Summaries for chat and channels

Overview

Teams Daily Summaries provide automated, concise recaps of the most important conversations and updates from your Microsoft Teams chats and channels. These summaries help you and your team stay aligned on key topics, decisions, and action items—without having to review every message. This feature is available to all Read AI users who connect Teams, including those on the Free plan, however Free users will need to log into Read regularly to re-enable their Ask Read AI setting, or else Read AI will stop processing new Teams messages. #### Prerequisites - Microsoft 365 or Teams Account: To use all features, you must use a work or school (not personal) Microsoft account. Personal Microsoft accounts (e.g., @outlook.com, @hotmail.com) can be connected but summaries will be limited to chats (no channels). - Read AI Account: Sign up for Read AI using Microsoft SSO or connect your Microsoft account after registration. - Connect Teams and Read AI: Connect your Read AI account with Microsoft Teams to enable the feature Permissions: Channel access requires admin approval (only needs to be approved once for the entire tenant)

How Teams Daily Summaries Work
  • Each day, Read AI analyzes new messages in the chats and channels from your connected Teams account.
  • Summaries highlight: Major discussion topics
  • Decisions and outcomes
  • Action items and owners
  • Notable announcements or updates
How You Can Receive Teams Daily Summaries

There are three ways to access your Teams Daily Summaries: 1. Read AI Web App (on the Reports Page) Once the Teams Sync to Read integration is connected, Daily Summaries automatically appear on your Reports page alongside meeting reports and Readout topics. 2. No additional setup is required, and this option cannot be disabled. 3. You can filter the Reports page to show or hide Daily Summaries from your list. 4. Direct Message (DM) from Read AI in Teams You can opt to receive your Daily Summary as a direct message from Read AI in Teams. 5. This setting is managed in the Push to Teams integration. 6. The DM covers the same content as the web app report, summarizing all channels and chats you have access to (organized by channel). 7. This option is user-specific and can be enabled or disabled at any time. 8. Channel Message from Read AI in Teams Read AI can post a summary directly to a Teams channel. 9. This summary includes only the activity from that specific channel. 10. Channel summaries can be enabled or disabled for each channel via the Push to Teams integration settings. 11. These settings are shared across all Read AI workspace members connected to the same Teams instance. 12. Any channel member, even those without a Read AI account, can opt out of receiving these summaries by clicking the opt-out link included in each summary message.

Setup Instructions
  1. Connect Teams to Read
  2. In the Read AI web app, navigate to Integrations > Microsoft Teams and connect your Microsoft account.
  3. You may need to get a Microsoft 365 admin to approve access to your Teams channels
  4. For more details, see How to Connect to Microsoft Teams
  5. Review your settings
  6. Personal Summaries controls whether you will receive a DM from Read AI in Teams with a recap that spans all of your chats and channels
  7. Channel Summaries controls which channels will have a summary of that channel, posted in the channel directly. The setting for each channel is shared by all Read AI users in the channel.
  8. Filtering and Managing Summaries
  9. Use filters on the Reports page to show or hide Daily Summaries.
  10. Channel summary messages can be opted out of by anyone in the channel via the link in the summary message (no Read AI account required).
Additional Details & Best Practices
  • Privacy: Summaries only include content from chats and channels you have access to.
  • Configurability: DMs and channel messages can be enabled or disabled as needed. Web app summaries are always available once Teams Sync is connected.
  • Workspace Settings: Channel summary settings apply to all workspace members connected to the same Teams instance.
  • Timing: Daily Summaries get generated around 8:00 AM, based on your account’s time zone setting (which can be updated under Account Settings > Profile & Account).
  • If Daily Summaries are enabled for a channel with Read AI users in different time zones, the most common time zone will get used.
  • Daily Summaries are not generated on Saturday or Sunday, but the Monday summary will cover any messages since the previous summary on Friday morning.
Frequently Asked Questions

Q: Can I disable Daily Summaries in the web app? A: No, but you can filter them out from your Reports page view. Q: Who receives channel summary messages? A: All members of the channel will see the summary, and anyone can opt out using the link provided in the message. Q: Can I customize which chats and channels are included in my summaries? A: DMs and web app reports summarize all accessible chats and channels, broken out by channel. Channel summary messages only include content from that channel.

Types of folders

Users can organize their Read AI meeting reports using two types of folders - personal folders and shared folders (Smart Folders are technically a personal folder). Custom folders are created and managed by users for personalized organization, while Smart Folders are automatically generated by Read AI to group reports based on detected topics. By default, any new folder you create is a custom folder. #### Personal Folders ##### Custom Folder A custom personal folder lets you organize your meeting reports according to your specific needs—whether by project, team, client, or any other criteria that suits your workflow. You have full control to create, name, and manage these folders, making it easy to keep related reports together in a way that works best for you. Your new folders are visible only to you, keeping your system private until you’re ready to share. - Personal folders are denoted by a “lock” icon - You can add any report to a personal folder, no matter whether if you’re an owner, editor or viewer of the report - Deleting Custom Folders: Deleting a custom folder is a permanent action and cannot be undone. You do not delete underlying report data by deleting a folder.

Smart Folders

Smart Folders are a type of personal folder, that automatically group your meeting reports by detected topics, so there’s no need for manual sorting. The names of Smart Folders are generated based on the topics they cover and cannot be edited. These folders dynamically update to include future reports that match their topic, helping you stay organized as new reports are added. - Smart Folders are denoted with a “star” icon - You can add any report to a Smart Folder, even as the folder continues to update automatically - Hiding Smart Folders: While you cannot delete Smart Folders, you can choose to hide any that aren’t relevant to you. Hidden Smart Folders can be restored at any time from the Account Settings > Folders page by unhiding them. This gives you flexibility to keep your folder list focused on what matters most.

Shared Folders

Shared Folders enable collaboration by allowing you to share a collection of reports with other individuals, teams, or entire workspaces. When you create a folder, you can designate it as either Personal or Shared. In a Shared Folder, all members receive the same access level (viewer or editor) to every report in the folder, regardless of their previous permissions. Shared Folders are ideal for team projects, onboarding, or any scenario where multiple people need access to the same set of reports. - Converting Folders: You can convert an existing personal folder to a shared folder at any time. However, only reports you own or have editor access to will be included in the shared folder. - Membership: Shared Folders can include individuals, teams, or workspaces as members, making it easy to manage access for dynamic groups.

Using Ask Read in Slack

Using Ask Read is a free, convenient, and powerful way to up your Slack game. After setting up the integration, you’ll be able to search, ask questions, and submit prompts to receive answers generated using not only your Slack messages, but also your meetings and any other integrations you have enabled for Ask Read. And with Read’s Slack app, you can do this all directly from within Slack. #### How to set up It only takes a few steps to set up: 1. Connect Push to Slack from your Integrations page.See Enabling Push to Slack for your account for details 2. Connect Slack Sync to Read from your Integrations page.While this isn’t technically required to use Ask Read from Slack, you will need this if you want your results to include your Slack messages. Otherwise you’ll only be searching over your meetings and other connected integrations. 3. Read AI should appear automatically under your Apps, but if not, you can just search for it (e.g. using ctrl+K on Windows/cmd+K on Mac) and then it will get added. Alternatively, you can go to https://slack.com/marketplace/A04UUCUGESK-read-ai and click on “Add to Slack” and then “Open in Slack”.

How to use

There are two ways to use Ask Read from Slack: 1. Navigate to the Read AI app (from the side navigation or search) and click on the “Chat” tab 2. Click on the Read AI icon from the Agents menu in the top right corner of Slack:

The agent view is nice because you can keep it open in a panel on the right side while you navigate around other channels and DMs. From either of these places, you can ask Read AI questions, just like you’re chatting with a person (it may take a moment to process and respond to each message). Please also note a couple of details: - This currently does not support ongoing chat threads, each query is separate (Read AI won’t remember context from your previous query). - All of your questions and answers will get saved in your search history on Read AI, unless you disable the search history setting.

For more information about Read’s Slack integration, see Connecting Read AI to Slack.

Using Read’s CRM Recommendations for HubSpot

Read’s CRM Recommendations feature leverages AI to help you keep your HubSpot pipeline up to date and moving forward. By analyzing your meetings, emails, and deal activity, Read proactively suggests when a deal in HubSpot is ready to advance to the next stage – saving you time, reducing manual updates, and helping you close more business. #### Key Benefits - Proactive AI guidance: Receive timely recommendations on which deals are ready to progress, based on real engagement signals. - Unified insights: Recommendations are powered by data from your meetings, emails, and HubSpot records—not just CRM activity alone. - Faster pipeline management: Keep your deals moving with less manual effort and fewer missed opportunities.

Prerequisites

  • Your Read account must be connected to HubSpot (see Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot).
  • You must have the appropriate permissions in HubSpot to view and update deals.
  • Deal stage recommendations are available on Read’s Pro and Enterprise plans.

How Deal Stage Recommendations Work

  • Read continuously monitors your HubSpot deals, relevant meetings, and email activity.
  • When the AI detects that a deal is likely ready for the next stage (e.g., after a positive meeting, completed action items, or key milestones), you’ll receive a recommendation.
  • Recommendations appear in your Read dashboard at the top of the Reports page

How to Use Deal Stage Recommendations

  1. Review Recommendations Go to the Reports page and check the box for “Your CRM Recommendations” to see if there are any new recommendations (make sure the box is expanded).
  2. You may see one or more deals listed with suggested next stages and a summary of the supporting evidence (e.g., recent meetings, sentiment, mentions of key phrases).
  3. Take Action If you agree with the recommendation, click the provided button to update the deal stage directly in HubSpot.
  4. If you disagree, click the dismiss button.
  5. Optionally, click on the deal name to view it in HubSpot, and add notes or comments for your team.
  6. Track Progress Updated deals will reflect the new stage immediately in HubSpot.
  7. Continue to monitor recommendations to keep your pipeline current.

Tips & Best Practices

  • Use recommendations as a starting point—always review the supporting context before updating a deal.
  • Share feedback on recommendations to help improve AI accuracy over time.
  • Combine with Ask Read to ask follow-up questions like “Which deals are stalled?” or “Show me deals ready to close this month.”

Troubleshooting

  • If you’re not seeing recommendations, ensure your HubSpot integration is active and the “Incorporate HubSpot Data into Read” setting is enabled.
  • Note that you will only get recommendations for deals that you own.
  • Some recommendations may not appear immediately after a meeting or email; allow time for data processing.
  • For access issues, check your Read plan and HubSpot permissions.

Additional Resources

  • Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot
  • Searching Your HubSpot Content with Ask Read

Using Read’s HubSpot UI Extension

Overview

Read’s HubSpot UI Extension brings the power of Ask Read and meeting insights directly into your HubSpot interface. By adding custom Read app cards to your HubSpot records, you can search across your entire knowledge base—including HubSpot, meetings, emails, and documents—directly from HubSpot. You can also play back videos for recent meetings related to your contacts, companies, or deals, streamlining your workflow and keeping all the context you need at your fingertips. #### Key Features - Ask Read Card: Run AI-powered searches and get answers from all your connected sources (HubSpot, meetings, emails, docs, and more). - Recent Meetings Card: Instantly view and play back recent meetings associated with the current HubSpot record, including participants, meeting summaries, and action items. - Flexible Placement: Add cards to the overview tab or side panel of any contact, company, or deal record. - Workspace-Level Setup: Admins can enable the extension for the entire workspace, ensuring consistent access and usage across the team.

Prerequisites

  • You must have the Read AI integration connected to your HubSpot account. (See  Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot for details.)
  • Being a Super Admin or having the “Customize record page layout” permission in HubSpot is required to add or configure app cards for your organization.
  • Your HubSpot data must be fully synced with Read to use search; you’ll receive an email notification when this is complete.

How to Enable the Read App Cards in HubSpot

  1. Log in to HubSpot as a Super Admin or user with the “Customize record page layout” permission.
  2. Go to Settings > Integrations > Connected Apps and select the Read AI app.
  3. Navigate to the App cards tab.
  4. Enable the Ask Read card and/or the Recent Meetings card for the record types you want (contacts, companies, deals).
  5. Choose where you want each card to appear: Overview Tab: Main content area for quick access.
  6. Side Panel: For a more compact, always-visible experience.
  7. Save your changes. The cards will now appear on the selected records for all users in your workspace.

Using the Ask Read Card

  • Enter your question or prompt in the Ask Read card (e.g., “Summarize all recent activity for this deal,” “Show open action items for this contact”).
  • The AI will return an answer using data from HubSpot, meetings, emails, and more.
  • Enable citations or expand the “Related Content” below the response to view full reports or sources cited by Ask Read.

Using the Recent Meetings Card

  • View a list of up to five recent meetings associated with the current record.
  • See meeting titles, dates, participants, and action items at a glance.
  • Play back meeting recordings and highlights directly within HubSpot (requires the Read Enterprise plan).
  • Expand to see more details or click through to the full meeting report in Read.
  • Note: this will only show meetings that you have access to in Read and also have been pushed to HubSpot either automatically or manually (i.e. as a “Meeting” record associated with a deal/contact/company).

Tips & Customization

  • Anyone with the “Customize record page layout” permission can add, remove, or rearrange cards at any time from the App cards tab.
  • Cards can be placed in either the overview tab or side panel, depending on your workflow preferences.
  • For best results, keep your Read integration and HubSpot data in sync.

Troubleshooting

  • If you don’t see the cards, check with your HubSpot admin to ensure the Read AI app and cards are enabled for your user role and record type.
  • If cards are not displaying content, confirm that you’re logged into Read, your integration is active and your HubSpot data is synced.
  • Some features (like video playback or searching premium integrations) may require additional permissions and/or a Read paid plan.

Additional Resources

  • Connecting Your Read Account to HubSpot
  • Searching Your HubSpot Content with Ask Read

What are “Flexible” Meetings?

For meetings that you host with internal participants only, you can mark the meeting as flexible on your Read AI calendar. You can do this from your Calendar page, or through the Read AI browser extension (Google Calendar only). On Read:(image: Screenshot 2025-09-16 at 2.36.28 PM.png) Browser extension: By setting a meeting to be flexible, Read automatically adjusts the time slot of the meeting to maximize focus time, high engagement, availability, and timeliness. Meetings will be rescheduled automatically (if a better time can be found) the night before the day they were originally scheduled on, to a different time that day.

What are folders?

Folders provide a powerful way to organize your meeting reports, enabling you to manage and analyze groups of reports in one convenient location. What is a folder? A folder is a collection of reports, and each report can belong to multiple folders. Folders can be created, named, and managed by users for personal organization or for sharing with others. There are two main types of folders: - Personal Folders: These folder are private to you, and ideal for personal organization. You can add any report to your personal folders, regardless of your permission level on that report (editor or viewer). You can create custom folders tailored to your needs, or take advantage of Smart Folders, which automatically group your reports by detected topics. - Shared Folders: Designed for team collaboration, shared folders allow you to add members—such as individuals, teams, or entire workspaces—so everyone in the folder can access and collaborate on all included reports. All members of a shared folder receive the same access level (viewer or editor) to every report in the folder.

What can a folder do? Folders can be used for both personal organization, collaborative knowledge sharing, or aggregated Ask Read AI insights across a collection of reports. Where are my folders? Users can access their folders directly from the main navigation on the left-hand side of the app. The folders page displays all folders you have access to—both personal and shared. #### Folders Page The folders page, accessible from the left-hand navigation, displays a list of all folders you have access to—both personal and shared. - By default, folders are sorted by the “Last updated” date, with the most recently updated folder appearing first. You can change the sorting order by clicking any column header—for example, click “Folder” to sort alphabetically by folder name. - The “Members” column indicates who has access to that folder, and subsequently, all of the reports in that folder. - The “Reports” column indicates the number of reports contained in each folder, giving you a quick overview of your folder contents.

Tag to Folder Migration

Prior to introducing Folders, we supported a feature called “Tags”. Any pre-existing custom tags have been seamlessly migrated to folders, so your existing organization remains intact—now with enhanced flexibility. The previous tag system has been fully replaced by folders across the user interface. Wherever you once saw custom tags, you’ll now see folders, including in the left navigation and report lists. - All existing custom tags have been converted into personal folders. You won’t lose any of your previously created tags—they have simply been transformed into folders, preserving both your organization and access. - The auto-tag feature has been deprecated and replaced by Smart Folders, which automatically group reports with greater accuracy based on detected topics.

Workspace tags have also been deprecated and converted into custom folders. You can continue to add reports to these folders created from the migration, ensuring a smooth transition and uninterrupted workflow.

What are report templates?

Report templates organize meeting reports into structured formats designed for different types of conversations and workflows, helping users move beyond generic summaries to more tailored, actionable outputs. Instead of relying on the same recap format for every meeting, you can use a template to highlight the information most relevant to you, such as key updates, customer needs, decisions, and next steps. Templates give you flexibility over how meeting information is organized, viewed, and shared. You can apply a template to your personal view without changing what others see, set a template as the shared view for everyone with access, create custom templates for your team’s workflows, and automatically push structured meeting insights to connected CRM systems. #### Pre-built report templates Read AI offers pre-built report templates for common meeting types and workflows. These include sales qualification frameworks such as SPICED, BANT, and MEDDPICC, as well as templates for discovery calls and one-on-ones. Additional templates will continue to be released. Select the template that best matches the meeting’s purpose to structure the report around the most relevant insights, decisions, and next steps for your meeting type. #### Custom templates Custom report templates let you define how information is organized in your meeting reports. You can create sections tailored to your organization’s processes, preferred frameworks, or CRM workflows, helping your team produce more consistent reports while reducing manual editing. You can create a custom template from scratch or use one of Read AI’s pre-built templates as a starting point.

What is focus time?

Focus time is the time in between meetings when you are able to get into the mindset of doing core work without the distraction of meetings. Read measures the time in between meetings by connecting to your calendar (Google Calendar, Microsoft Outlook, and/or Zoom) to identify a minimum of two hours between meetings and labels that time “focus time”. Read does not schedule focus time on your calendar explicitly, rather it is calculated behind the scenes and will evolve dynamically as your schedule changes. Focus time is one of the key factors used by the Read Smart Scheduler to help you schedule your time more efficiently in a few different ways: - When scheduling meetings on your Google Calendar with the Read Smart Scheduler (Read’s browser extension), Read will recommend available slots that optimize focus time (as well as engagement and timeliness) - Similarly, the same will occur when scheduling meetings using your Read Smart Scheduler scheduling link - If a meeting is marked as “flexible” on your calendar, then Read will automatically reschedule it to a time that optimizes focus time (as well as engagement and timeliness)

Read will automatically consider not only your own schedule and behavioral metrics, but also that of your other meeting guests, as long as they also have a Read account.

What kind of recommendations should I expect to see in Read Smart Scheduler?

Upcoming meetings will have recommendations to change invitees to optional, remove invitees from the meeting, or review the meeting series altogether based on key factors. Key factors are observations Read has made from past meetings, these can include conversation balance and turn taking, participant engagement, whether the tone of the meeting or participant is negative, and attendee timeliness. It’s easy to take action on recommendations as the ones you accept are automatically reflected in the meeting event.

Getting Started with Read

Admitting and Removing Read from a Meeting

Read AI was founded with privacy as a priority, and uses industry best practices around security and privacy. All data measured during video conference calls are encrypted during transmission and storage, and is, by default, protected behind our account login system. Read does not publish data to the open internet, does not sell user data, does not use it to improve its proprietary models unless a user has opted-in, and will not share user data with third parties except as necessary to perform the functions of our business. The owner of a report also has full control over who the content is shared with, if at all. But if you still do not want Read to join a meeting you are in, there are several steps that can be taken. #### Configuring Read to join a meeting (Read users only) If auto-join is enabled and you decide at the last minute that you do not want it to join a meeting, the easiest way to prevent it from joining is to visit your Read Calendar, find the meeting, and toggle off the “Add Read?” button. As long as you do this a few minutes before the meeting scheduled start time, Read should have time to sync and know not to join it. #### Admitting Read (host only, depending on platform) Read will typically ask to be admitted to a meeting before joining (dependent on meeting platform settings). In most cases, the host or another participant will see a request from Read asking to be admitted. If they do not want Read to join, they can simply deny the request. ##### For Microsoft Teams ##### For Google Meet For Google Meet, Read has the ability to join either as an AI meeting agent or via Google Meet’s Native recording integration. ###### Joining as an Agent You will first get a notification saying someone wants to join that can look a couple different ways. Click “View” and you’ll be taken to the People screen, where you can admit or deny Read from joining. To deny, click on the three dots next to the Notetaker and select “Deny entry”. If Read is the only one there, you can also click “Deny all.” ###### Joining with Google Meet’s Native Recording When Read joins a meeting using Google Meet’s native recording integration, you (or a meeting participant) will see a pop-up notification at the scheduled start time. Someone will need to confirm that all participants have given consent by clicking “Start Read Chief Meeting Officer”. If the call is hosted in a Google Workspace, a user from that Workspace will need to approve this pop-up. If the call is hosted by a gmail.com user, then whoever invited Read AI will need to confirm the dialog. ##### For Zoom NOTE: if the host has previously installed the Read app in Zoom, or connected their Zoom account to Read, this request to admit will not appear. That is because Zoom would have already assigned Read a token that lets it skip the request and join the meeting. #### Removing Read (host only) You might notice that Read joins meetings as a participant (it has its own square the way other participants do). This is by design, so that if Read was admitted to the meeting, but needs to be removed, the host can remove it the same way they would a participant. ##### For Microsoft Teams In Teams, click on the Participants icon. Once in that window, find the notetaker, and right click on it to open the options menu. Then select “remove from meeting.” No further confirmation is required, and Read will be removed from the meeting. ##### For Google Meet ###### Agent: Removing a participant from Google Meet is a multi step process. First, click on the “People” icon.  Once in that window, find the notetaker, and click on the three dots next to it. Select “remove from the call”. You will then be asked to confirm that the participants should be removed. Click “Remove” again, and the Read AI agent will leave the call. ###### Native Integration: The host, as well as any meeting participant, can manually stop recording by clicking the Read AI logo at the top right and then clicking “Stop sharing.” This will make Read stop recording and generate a report with the content recorded up to that point. ##### For Zoom Removing a participant from Zoom is also a multi step process. First, click on the “Participants” icon. Once in that window, find the notetaker, and click on the three dots next to it. Select “remove from the call”. You will then have to confirm you want it removed by clicking “Remove”, and the agent will leave the call. #### Removing Read (any meeting participant) As a best practice, Read asks users to notify participants they they are recording the meeting. When available, Read AI will also send out an automatic notification that is posted in the meeting chat when Read joins a meeting, which includes: - notification of recording - the name of the user(s) who invited it - options to remove it usingchat commands or by clicking on an opt out link

Any participant in the meeting can remove Read at any point by typing the commands into the meeting chat, or by clicking on the provided link. If you’re using chat commands, enter them without the quotation marks, and they will take effect immediately: - “Read Stop” = Read will leave the meeting and generate a report based on the data measured before this command - “Opt Out” = Read will leave the meeting and delete all measured data (no report will be generated) Note, if an opt out link is provided you must click on the link to opt out. The chat command will not work.

For Microsoft Teams

Unlike Google Meet and Zoom, Read sends an opt-out link that allows participants to opt out. Note that if you see an opt out link provided, you must use the link or remove Read as a meeting participant to remove Read from the meeting - chat commands will not work. ##### For Google Meet Joining as an Agent: Any meeting participant can type: 1. “Read stop” in the chat and send it to remove Read. A meeting report would be generated with the recorded content up to that point. 2. “Opt out” in the chat and send it to remove Read. All recorded content would be deleted and no meeting report would be generated.

Joining via Native Integration: Since Read does not join as an agent, no chat commands will be used. Rather, any meeting participant can manually stop recording by clicking the Read AI logo at the top right and then clicking “Stop sharing.” This will make Read stop recording and generate a report with the content recorded up to that point. To opt out during a meeting, click on the 9-dot grid on the bottom right corner, then look for the Read AI app, open it. Click the Read AI logo at the bottom left. Select the “Opt out” option. This will remove Read from the meeting and delete any recorded content. ##### For Zoom Any meeting participant can type: 1. “Read stop” in the chat and send it to remove Read. A meeting report would be generated with the recorded content up to that point. 2. “Opt out” in the chat and send it to remove Read. All recorded content would be deleted and no meeting report would be generated.

NOTE: If you were late to the meeting, you might not be able to see the chat notification unless the host pins it to the chat. But these chat commands are always available. Since the name of the user(s) who invited Read to the meeting will also be listed, you can always reach out to them and request that they do not use Read when meeting with you. We provide all these options to remove Read from meetings because we believe that building Read with privacy, transparency, and user control at its core encourages more authentic conversations.

Can I use Read’s mobile app to record calls from my phone?

Read AI does not currently support recording a phone call, FaceTime, or virtual meeting via our mobile apps. For context, if another application is using your microphone, the Read app will not be able to supersede that and use your microphone to capture audio for the purposes of generating a Read meeting report. You can use the Read AI apps for Android and iOS for in-person meetings, brainstorm sessions, and more - just make sure that your microphone is not already in use elsewhere. If you’ll be joining a Teams, Google Meet, or Zoom meeting from your phone, invite Read to the meeting by enabling auto-join from your account settings ahead of the call.

Can Read join a meeting if I don’t attend?

Yes, Read can join your meetings, even if you don’t attend. This is a great use case for someone who gets double- or triple-booked, or had a last-minute conflict. However, you must have first been invited to the meeting, and the host must have admit Read and allow recording. - What meetings does Read join when I connect my calendar? - I don’t want Read to join all my meetings, what are my options? - Why didn’t Read join my meeting?

Capturing WebEx Meetings with Read (Desktop App)

While WebEx is not a supported platform, Read can support WebEx meetings through our desktop application, allowing you to capture meeting insights. #### How it works Read does not join or record Webex meetings as an agent. Instead, the desktop app captures audio from your system while you’re in the meeting, allowing Read to generate a report with transcripts, notes, and action items. #### Best practices - If you’re wearing headphones, make sure the correct output device is selected in both your system and WebEx settings - Confirm the Read desktop app has permission to capture system audio - Avoid switching audio devices mid-meeting, as this can interrupt audio capture
